3196               	;
3197               	; 	' THE JUMP ARC IS A TABLE, not integration. That makes the 14 px apex a
3198               	; 	' property of the data rather than of a fixed-point velocity that has to
3199               	; 	' be tuned, and it keeps every comparison in the jump 8-bit and unsigned.
3200               	; 	' DESIGN.md 5a depends on the apex being exactly 14.
3201               	; 	'
3202               	; 	' THIRTY FRAMES WITH NINE OF THEM AT THE APEX. The first version was 24
3203               	; 	' frames that touched 14 for only four, which made every jump a timing
3204               	; 	' test rather than a decision -- you had to leave the ground on exactly
3205               	; 	' the right frame or clip the thing you were jumping. Widening the
3206               	; 	' plateau rather than raising the apex keeps the ball arithmetic in
3207               	; 	' DESIGN.md 5a intact (the apex is what that depends on) while making the
3208               	; 	' window forgiving: the player still has to CHOOSE to jump, but no longer
3209               	; 	' has to be frame-perfect about it.
3210               	; 	' THE ARC IS A TABLE, not thirty assignments -- each of those compiled to
3211               	; 	' several bytes of code, and the fixed area is the binding budget.
3212               	; 	#jat = VARPTR jarc_tbl(0)
3213 AE4E 0200  20 	li r0,cvb_JARC_TBL
     AE50 7C58     
3214 AE52 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__JAT
     AE54 20A4

3264 AECC C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
3265 AECE 0450  20 	b *r0
3266               	;
3267               	; 	' THE FONT'S COLOUR TABLE WAS 472 BYTES OF ONE REPEATED VALUE, which is a
3268               	; 	' fifth of what the fixed area had left. DEFINE COLOR copies such a table
3269               	; 	' out of ROM into all three screen thirds; writing the constant straight
3270               	; 	' into the colour table does the same job for the price of a loop, and
3271               	; 	' the loop runs once, at boot.
3272               	; 	'
3273               	; 	' The colour table is at >2000 and the VDP mirrors it once per screen
3274               	; 	' third, >800 apart. Both numbers were read out of the GENERATED assembly
3275               	; 	' -- define_color's `ai r0,>2000` and LDIRVM3's `ai r0,>0800` -- rather
3276               	; 	' than assumed, because a wrong base here would paint over the pattern
3277               	; 	' table and the failure would look like corrupt artwork.
3278               	; 	'
3279               	; 	' Paced: a few hundred VDP writes in one frame are silently dropped.
3280               	; 	' THE SCANNER'S COLOURS ARE THREE BLOCKS REPEATED SIXTEEN TIMES EACH, so
3281               	; 	' the table shipped 384 bytes to say 24 bytes' worth. Same trick as the
3282               	; 	' font: write the colour table directly. >2000 is the colour table's base
3283               	; 	' and >800 the stride between screen thirds -- the same layout DEFINE COLOR
3284               	; 	' walks for us in setup_font, which this cannot use because it is cheaper to
3285               	; 	' expand 24 bytes here than to ship 384 in the bank.
3286               	; 	' NOTHING IS UNDER THE GROUND FLOOR, SO NOTHING SHOULD BE GREEN THERE.
3287               	; 	' A floor bar is five pixels of yellow over three of the green air
3288               	; 	' belonging to the floor BELOW it (see SLAB), which is right for three of
3289               	; 	' the four bars and wrong for the last one: below floor 0 there is no
3290               	; 	' floor, only the scanner, and the green read as a strip of shop with
3291               	; 	' nothing in it.
3292               	; 	'
3293               	; 	' NO NEW CHARACTER IS NEEDED, BECAUSE THE COLOUR TABLE HAS THREE COPIES
3294               	; 	' -- one per eight screen rows -- and the bands land such that each bar
3295               	; 	' sits in a different third:
3296               	; 	'
3297               	; 	'     band 0 roof   rows  1-5    bar row  5   third 0
3298               	; 	'     band 1        rows  6-10   bar row 10   third 1
3299               	; 	'     band 2        rows 11-15   bar row 15   third 1
3300               	; 	'     band 3 GROUND rows 16-20   bar row 20   third 2
3301               	; 	'
3302               	; 	' so recolouring SLAB in third 2 alone reaches the ground floor's bar and
3303               	; 	' nothing else. The same third also holds the scanner, but the scanner
3304               	; 	' does not use these characters. This is the same mechanism that once hid
3305               	; 	' a bug for months (CLAUDE.md 3A, the blanked-in-one-third note) used
3306               	; 	' deliberately for once.
3307               	; 	'
3308               	; 	' Only the three bottom lines change, and only their BACKGROUND: SLAB has
3309               	; 	' no ink there and SLABE's brick keeps its grey.
3310               	; floor0_colour:
3311               cvb_FLOOR0_COLOUR
3312               	; 	' TWO CHARACTERS, THE SAME THREE SCAN LINES, ONE ROUTINE. Both blocks
3313               	; 	' were written out in full and differed only in which character and which
3314               	; 	' colour byte -- eleven statements twice, including the multiply-by-eight
3315               	; 	' done by doubling (`*` compiles to a real TMS9900 MPY, which clobbers r0,
3316               	; 	' CLAUDE.md 3A).
3317               	; 	f0c = CH_SLAB
3318 AED0 0200  20 	li r0,24576   ; 96*256
     AED2 6000     
3319 AED4 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0C
     AED6 20CE     
3320               	; 	f0v = 177			' LYELL on BLACK -- no ink, so the
3321 AED8 0200  20 	li r0,45312   ; 177*256
     AEDA B100     
3322 AEDC D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0V
     AEDE 20D6     
3323               	; 	GOSUB f0_rows			' background is all that shows
3324 AEE0 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     AEE2 A980     
3325 AEE4 AF00     	data cvb_F0_ROWS
3326               	; 	f0c = CH_SLABE
3327 AEE6 0200  20 	li r0,24832   ; 97*256
     AEE8 6100     
3328 AEEA D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0C
     AEEC 20CE     
3329               	; 	f0v = 225			' GRAY brick on BLACK
3330 AEEE 0200  20 	li r0,57600   ; 225*256
     AEF0 E100     
3331 AEF2 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0V
     AEF4 20D6     
3332               	; 	GOSUB f0_rows
3333 AEF6 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     AEF8 A980     
3334 AEFA AF00     	data cvb_F0_ROWS
3335               	; 	RETURN

3677               	; 	' The NOTICE is redrawn on every title visit even though the MEASUREMENT
3678               	; 	' happens once -- it is information about the machine, and the CLS above
3679               	; 	' just wiped it.

3681 B280 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
3682 B282 0450  20 	b *r0
3683               	;
3684               	; 	' NO ALPHA LOCK CALIBRATION, AND THERE USED TO BE ONE.
3685               	; 	'
3686               	; 	' The premise was that ALPHA LOCK shares a line with the joystick's
3687               	; 	' VERTICAL axis, so a latched key reports a direction that is never
3688               	; 	' released -- fatal here, where down is the duck and up is the lift. The
3689               	; 	' guard sampled the axis for forty frames before listening and then
3690               	; 	' ignored any direction that had been held for essentially all of them.
3691               	; 	'
3692               	; 	' IT WAS REMOVED BECAUSE THE PREMISE DOES NOT HOLD UP.
3693               	; 	'
3694               	; 	'   * CLAUDE.md claimed every other game here dodged the problem by not
3695               	; 	'     reading up/down. EIGHT of them do read it -- Adventire, Astiroids,
3696               	; 	'     HardHatMack, Ms. Pac-Man, RallyX, Structris, UFO, Bust-A-Bobble --
3697               	; 	'     and not one has a calibration or has ever shown the fault.
3698               	; 	'   * It never fired on the machine this is developed on: the notice it
3699               	; 	'     prints when it detects a stuck axis has never appeared.
3700               	; 	'   * The original evidence was that the game "came up and would not
3701               	; 	'     start" -- and the first version BLOCKED until the axis cleared. That
3702               	; 	'     symptom is indistinguishable from the several input bugs since found
3703               	; 	'     and fixed for real: keys arriving at a screen that was not listening
3704               	; 	'     yet. The diagnosis was probably one of those.
3705               	; 	'
3706               	; 	' It also cost two bugs of its own: forty frames of a drawn but deaf title
3707               	; 	' screen, which swallowed the first digit of 8-3-8, and a wrong theory
3708               	; 	' about keyboard noise that led to a filter which ate real presses.
3709               	; 	'
3710               	; 	' If a stuck axis ever does appear it will be obvious -- the player will
3711               	; 	' duck or ride the lift without asking -- and the guard is in the history.
3712               	;
3713               	; title_input:
3714               cvb_TITLE_INPUT
3715               	; 	' THE PROMPT IS PRINTED HERE, NOT WITH THE REST OF THE TITLE, because
3716               	; 	' this is the moment the game starts listening -- and it is a whole
3717               	; 	' second after the title appears.
3718               	; 	'
3719               	; 	' title_draw runs early on purpose (DESIGN.md 0d-nonies) so the screen
3720               	; 	' arrives instead of filling in, but setup_rest, init_tables and the
3721               	; 	' ALPHA LOCK sample all run AFTER it. For about a second the title is up,
3722               	; 	' finished, and completely deaf. A player types 8-3-8 into that window and
3723               	; 	' the first digit lands in nothing -- then a following 3-8 completes the
3724               	; 	' code, because the 8 they typed second is still standing as state. That
3725               	; 	' is exactly how it was reported, twice, and it is not noise: it is the
3726               	; 	' screen lying about being ready.
3727               	; 	'
3728               	; 	' RallyX and Bust-A-Bobble never showed this because they draw their
3729               	; 	' titles when they are already listening. Keystone cannot -- the whole
3730               	; 	' point of the early draw is that it is early -- so the PROMPT waits
3731               	; 	' instead, and its arrival is the cue that the screen is awake.
3732               	; 	PRINT AT 614,"FIRE TO START"

3862               	;
3863               	; 	' 8-3-8 IS EDGE-TRIGGERED AND ANY STRAY DIGIT RESETS IT (see title_wait).
3864               	; 	'
3865               	; 	' It used to read cont1.key raw every pass and test only for the digit it
3866               	; 	' wanted next. That worked -- but only because 8-3-8 ALTERNATES, so holding
3867               	; 	' 8 cannot advance past the first step. It is an accident of the sequence
3868               	; 	' rather than a design, and it had two costs: 8,5,3,9,8 opened this page as
3869               	; 	' readily as 8,3,8, and a key that READS as held for many frames got a free
3870               	; 	' walk through the state machine.
3871               	; 	'
3872               	; 	' That second one is not hypothetical on this machine. ALPHA LOCK shorts a
3873               	; 	' keyboard line -- and Classic99 defaults to invertcaps, so it reads DOWN
3874               	; 	' with the host's Caps Lock UP -- and this page was reached twice from
3875               	; 	' single keypresses while testing an unrelated change.
3876               	; 	'
3877               	; 	' The reset is the DEFAULT rather than a test for a particular wrong digit:
3878               	; 	' Bust-A-Bobble resets only on a stray 3 and still lets 8,5,3,8 through.
3879               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------- 838 setup page
3880               	; setup838:

3886               	; 	' THEY ARE TYPED. There is no heading and no instructions: a page showing
3887               	; 	' exactly one question does not need to explain that one digit answers it,
3888               	; 	' and the second question does not exist until the first is answered.
3889               	; 	'
3890               	; 	' Showing the CURRENT values first was the obvious thing and it cost more
3891               	; 	' than it gave -- two draw routines, a "fire keeps what is shown" escape to
3892               	; 	' make the display mean something, and the defaults had to be applied here
3893               	; 	' as well as in new_game so the page had numbers to show at all. None of it
3894               	; 	' survives: every digit on screen is one the player just typed. Four
3895               	; 	' strings became two, a two-field loop became a straight line, and the page

4272               	; 	' THE FIRE THAT STARTED THE ROUND IS NOT ALSO A JUMP. jrel is the jump's
4273               	; 	' release latch -- the button has to come UP between jumps -- and it was
4274               	; 	' never reset when play began, so it carried its value in from whatever
4275               	; 	' happened last. Press FIRE on the title with jrel left at 1 and Kelly
4276               	; 	' jumps on the first frame of the round, having been told to do so by the
4277               	; 	' keypress that only meant "start".
4278               	; 	'
4279               	; 	' It hid on the very first game of a session, because CVBasic zeroes its
4280               	; 	' variables and jrel = 0 already means "wait for a release" -- so it only
4281               	; 	' appeared on the SECOND round onwards, which reads as intermittent.
4282               	; 	'
4283               	; 	' start_krook is the one place worth doing this: new_game, losing a life
4284               	; 	' and advancing a Krook all pass through here, so every entry into play
4285               	; 	' demands a fresh press.
4286               	; 	jrel = 0
4287 B680 04C0  14 	clr r0

     B684 2095

     B68A 20FD     
4292               	;
4293               	; 	' HARRY STARTS ON FLOOR 2 AT THE WEST EDGE OF SCREEN 7, and that is as
4294               	; 	' far left as the clock allows. The research says "the second-floor
4295               	; 	' elevator door", which is screen 3, and it cannot be done:
4296               	; 	'
4297               	; 	'   spawn      escape     round
4298               	; 	'   screen 3   119.9 s    100 s   -- impossible
4299               	; 	'   screen 5   108.1 s    100 s   -- impossible
4300               	; 	'   screen 6   102.3 s    100 s   -- impossible
4301               	; 	'   screen 7    96.4 s    100 s   -- 3.6 s of room
4302               	; 	'
4303               	; 	' HE STARTS AT THE LIFT NOW, AND THAT COST HIM A SPEED CHANGE.
4304               	; 	'
4305               	; 	' His escape route ZIGZAGS -- east along floor 2 to its escalator, WEST
4306               	; 	' along floor 3 to that one, then east again along the roof to the door.
4307               	; 	' From the lift on screen 3 that is 5,120 px against the 4,208 he walked
4308               	; 	' from screen 7, so at his old 1.75 px a pass he needed 105 s of a 100 s
4309               	; 	' round: he could never escape at all, and one of the two loss conditions
4310               	; 	' would quietly have stopped existing. This block used to say exactly that
4311               	; 	' and conclude the lift was impossible. It was impossible AT THAT SPEED.
4312               	; 	'
4313               	; 	' AND QUARTER-PIXELS COULD NOT EXPRESS THE ANSWER. A quarter of a pixel
4314               	; 	' per pass is worth about TEN SECONDS of his route, so the two candidate
4315               	; 	' values straddled the buzzer with nothing in between:
4316               	; 	'
4317               	; 	'   2.00 px/pass   escapes at 102.4 s of a 100 s round   never escapes
4318               	; 	'   2.25 px/pass   escapes at  91.0 s                    nine seconds early
4319               	; 	'
4320               	; 	' Worse, 2.25 was not reachable at all: the accumulator below had two
4321               	; 	' drain steps and therefore a hard ceiling of 2 px a pass, so hsp4 = 9
4322               	; 	' silently delivered 2.00 and he fell two thirds of a screen short of his
4323               	; 	' own escape. See the accumulator comment for the full account.
4324               	; 	'
4325               	; 	' Sixteenths with three drains put the step at about 2.5 s instead of 10,
4326               	; 	' and hsp64 = 35 (2.1875 px/pass) lands him at 96.3 s of the 100 --
4327               	; 	' escaping as the timer expires, which is what was asked for.
4328               	; 	'
4329               	; 	' 35 AND NOT 34, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE PLAYER'S OWN SCREEN. Measured
4330               	; 	' in play at 34: standing on an ordinary screen he escaped with one timer
4331               	; 	' unit left, and standing on the ELEVATOR screen he ran out of clock --
4332               	; 	' same route, same constant, decided by where the player happened to be.
4333               	; 	' Everything that moves is paced per loop PASS while the clock is paced in
4334               	; 	' real FRAMES, so a busy screen slows Harry in real time and the clock does
4335               	; 	' not slow with him. A whole round measured 2,335 passes over about 98 s --
4336               	; 	' 23.8 a second, not the 25 the model assumes -- and the heavy screens are
4337               	; 	' slower again. 35 buys the margin that covers the worst of them. The
4338               	; 	' underlying split is recorded in DESIGN.md 0f-ter; this constant only
4339               	; 	' papers over it.
4340               	; 	'
4341               	; 	' The margin
4342               	; 	' that remains is the ROUND's, not the chase's: Kelly still has to get
4343               	; 	' there first, and does. assets/checkchase.py walks both routes out of
4344               	; 	' these constants, models the drain ceiling, and fails the build if the

4561               	;
4562               	; 	' ======================================================================
4563               	; 	' DRAWING THE STORE -- four blits, once per screen crossing
4564               	; 	' ======================================================================
4565               	; 	' This is the whole cost of the flip, and it is paid on a discrete event
4566               	; 	' rather than every frame. RallyX's pan repaints 576 chars every time the
4567               	; 	' camera moves one cell; this repaints 640 about once every two seconds
4568               	; 	' of running.
4569               	; 	' SPRITES OFF BEFORE THE BLIT, NOT AFTER IT. This routine WAITs between
4570               	; 	' bands -- one band a frame, because a burst of VDP writes past a few
4571               	; 	' dozen in one frame is silently dropped -- so painting the new screen
4572               	; 	' takes several frames, and for all of them the OLD screen's actors and
4573               	; 	' obstacles were still sitting on top of it. Following Harry through a
4574               	; 	' seam showed him and every cart from the floor he had just left,
4575               	; 	' standing on a shop that was assembling itself underneath them.
4576               	; 	'
4577               	; 	' They are put back by the normal draw on the same pass, so the crossing
4578               	; 	' reads as a cut rather than as a dissolve with ghosts in it.
4579               	; 	' EACH BAND IS FINISHED BEFORE THE FRAME IT IS DRAWN IN ENDS, and the
4580               	; 	' bands go TOP DOWN. Both halves of that matter.
4581               	; 	'
4582               	; 	' The blit paints the template -- shelves, pillars and all -- and the
4583               	; 	' radios and prizes then take out the fixtures they stand in. Those two
4584               	; 	' used to be four frames apart: all four bands blitted with a WAIT
4585               	; 	' between them, and only afterwards the clearing. So every screen change
4586               	; 	' showed its counters and beams for several frames and then erased them,
4587               	; 	' which reads as the shop flickering rather than as it being drawn.
4588               	; 	'
4589               	; 	' TOP DOWN because a band's beam tops are stamped into the SLAB ROW OF
4590               	; 	' THE BAND ABOVE. Going bottom-up, band 1's blit would paint over the
4591               	; 	' beam tops band 0 had just put there. Going top-down the band above is
4592               	; 	' always already drawn, so everything a band needs -- its own cells and
4593               	; 	' the row it borrows overhead -- is final by the time its WAIT arrives.
4594               	; 	'
4595               	; 	' `3 - dq` rather than `FOR dlv = 3 TO 0 STEP -1`: dlv is an unsigned
4596               	; 	' byte, so counting down past zero wraps to 255 and the loop never ends
4597               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A).
4598               	; draw_screen:
4599               cvb_DRAW_SCREEN
4600               	; 	GOSUB hide_play
4601 B800 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B802 A980     
4602 B804 EA50     	data cvb_HIDE_PLAY

4604 B806 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B808 A980     
4605 B80A BBF4     	data cvb_LOAD_BAND
4606               	; 	FOR dq = 0 TO 3
4607 B80C 04C0  14 	clr r0
4608 B80E D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_DQ
     B810 21B3     
4609               cv119
4610               	; 		' WAIT FIRST, NOT LAST. A band is blitted and then corrected -- the
4611               	; 		' beam top stamped on, the pillar under a radio taken off -- and the
4612               	; 		' raster does not stop while that happens. Starting the burst in the
4613               	; 		' middle of a frame let the raster pass the band between the blit and
4614               	; 		' the correction, so the uncorrected version was shown for a frame:
4615               	; 		' at round start, after CLS, that reads as a support beam appearing
4616               	; 		' and being rubbed out. Beginning at vblank gives the whole burst a
4617               	; 		' clear run at the rows before the beam reaches them. It also still
4618               	; 		' gives each band its own frame, which is what stops a VDP write
4619               	; 		' burst past a few dozen from being silently dropped.
4620               	; 		WAIT
4621 B812 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B814 A980

4708               	;
4709               	; 	' A SUPPORT BEAM HAS TO REACH THE FLOOR IT HOLDS UP, AND THAT FLOOR IS
4710               	; 	' NOT IN ITS OWN BAND. A beam fills its band's four air rows, so its top
4711               	; 	' pixel sits under the slab row of the band ABOVE -- and a slab row is
4712               	; 	' five pixels of bar over three of green (see SLAB), so the beam stopped
4713               	; 	' three pixels short of the bar on every storey. It read as a beam that
4714               	; 	' misses the floor, which is exactly what it was.
4715               	; 	'
4716               	; 	' It cannot be drawn into either template. The beam belongs to the band
4717               	; 	' below and the row belongs to the band above, and the two templates are
4718               	; 	' chosen independently per screen, so no template knows both. So the
4719               	; 	' band blits go down first and the tops are stamped afterwards, from a
4720               	; 	' per-template column table -- the same shape as the escalator's head cap.
4721               	; 	'
4722               	; 	' Bands 0 and 1 only. Band 2's top row is the ROOF DECK, which is grey
4723               	; 	' over grey with no green to bridge, and the roof itself has no band
4724               	; 	' above it at all.
4725               	; 	' BANDS 0 TO 2 -- ALL THREE SHOPPING FLOORS. It used to stop at 1,
4726               	; 	' because band 2's ceiling is the ROOF DECK and the deck had no green
4727               	; 	' under it: a beam there already ran straight into grey and needed
4728               	; 	' nothing. Giving the roof its three green rows (so the lift shaft stops
4729               	; 	' under it, like every other floor) took that away, and the top floor's
4730               	; 	' beams started falling three pixels short.
4731               	; 	'
4732               	; 	' So the roof gets its own version of the stamp. A beam HOLDS THE ROOF
4733               	; 	' UP and must reach it; the shaft merely stops beneath it.
4734               	; beam_one:
4735               cvb_BEAM_ONE
4736               	; 	IF bt > 2 THEN RETURN		' the roof has no band above it

5269               	; 			' AND HOW MANY FLOORS CARRY ONE AT ALL. The gates above
5270               	; 			' decide what KIND a hazard is; this decides whether the
5271               	; 			' floor has one.
5272               	; 			'
5273               	; 			' The kind-arrival rule downgrades anything that has not
5274               	; 			' arrived yet to a beach ball rather than leaving the floor
5275               	; 			' empty, so that a floor is never bare -- and the effect on
5276               	; 			' Krook 1 was a ball on all FOUR floors of every populated
5277               	; 			' screen. All four bands are on screen at once, so that is
5278               	; 			' four identical balls in view at all times, which is not
5279               	; 			' what "merely a few beach balls" describes.
5280               	; 			'
5281               	; 			' Per-band count was never the problem: only one slot is
5282               	; 			' live until Krook 6. It is the number of OCCUPIED FLOORS,
5283               	; 			' and it had no ramp at all.
5284               	; 			'
5285               	; 			' Floors 1 and 3 stay clear on Krook 1, floor 1 joins on
5286               	; 			' Krook 2, and the roof on Krook 3. ALTERNATING rather than
5287               	; 			' clearing the top or bottom half, so the empty floors do
5288               	; 			' not stack into a visibly dead region that reads as a bug
5289               	; 			' -- and the ROOF is last, because that is where the round
5290               	; 			' is decided.

5329               	; 			' NO SPRITE HAZARD ON THE FLOOR THE CROOK IS STANDING ON,
5330               	; 			' AND THE DECISION IS MADE HERE -- once, as the screen is
5331               	; 			' drawn, and never revisited while it is on screen.
5332               	; 			'
5333               	; 			' It was a test in the draw loop and another in the
5334               	; 			' collision test, both re-evaluated every frame, so the
5335               	; 			' hazards came and went as Harry walked on and off the
5336               	; 			' screen: run him to the edge and a floor's worth of balls
5337               	; 			' appeared out of nothing behind him. A rule about what
5338               	; 			' this screen CONTAINS cannot be a per-frame question --
5339               	; 			' the answer changes while the player is looking at it.
5340               	; 			'
5341               	; 			' Zeroing the kind here also makes both of those tests
5342               	; 			' unnecessary: an obstacle that was never loaded cannot be
5343               	; 			' drawn and cannot hit anybody.
5344               	; 			'
5345               	; 			' Radios are exempt. They are characters rather than
5346               	; 			' sprites, they do not move, and a fixture the crook runs
5347               	; 			' past is not what this rule is about.
5348               	; 			IF lk <> OB_RADIO THEN

     BDC0 21DE

     BDC4 21DC

     BDCA 2288     
5379 BDCC D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5380               	; 			' A RADIO NEEDS A CELL, NOT A PIXEL, because it is drawn as
5381               	; 			' characters. Divided by repeated subtraction: `/` compiles
5382               	; 			' to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A), and this runs once
5383               	; 			' per screen where the divide would run per obstacle -- so
5384               	; 			' the loop is the cheaper of the two, and the only one that
5385               	; 			' cannot be got wrong by a rounding rule.
5386               	; 			obc(li) = 0
5387 BDCE 04C1  14 	clr r1
5388 BDD0 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LI,r0
     BDD2 21DC     
5389 BDD4 0980  32 	srl r0,8
5390 BDD6 0220  22 	ai r0,array_OBC
     BDD8 2270     
5391 BDDA D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5392               	; 			' PER KIND, not one global speed (see start_krook).
5393               	; 			' THE SPEED IS A PROPERTY OF THE KIND, and obs() only ever
5394               	; 			' held a copy of it -- set from the kind here, zeroed for a
5395               	; 			' radio below, and read in one place. upd_obst derives it
5396               	; 			' from obk() instead, which is 8 bytes of RAM back and one
5397               	; 			' fewer thing that can disagree with itself.
5398               	; 			' THEY COME FROM THE FAR SIDE. Kelly walked into this screen
5399               	; 			' from one edge, so the obstacles start at the OTHER edge and
5400               	; 			' run toward him. Crossing a seam then always presents an
5401               	; 			' ONCOMING stream, never a set of backs he has to catch up
5402               	; 			' with -- and it means the direction he is travelling is
5403               	; 			' always the direction the danger comes from.
5404               	; 			'
5405               	; 			' The table's x becomes a stagger from that edge rather than
5406               	; 			' an absolute position, and the slot index spreads them out
5407               	; 			' so they arrive in sequence instead of as one wall.
5408               	; 			'
5409               	; 			' THE SECOND ONE IS A FIXED DISTANCE BEHIND THE FIRST, AND
5410               	; 			' THAT DISTANCE IS SET BY THE JUMP.
5411               	; 			'
5412               	; 			' It used to be `(lx AND 63) + 64` -- a masked byte out of
5413               	; 			' the placement table plus a constant -- so the gap between
5414               	; 			' the two hazards was whatever those two bytes happened to
5415               	; 			' differ by. Measured across the five populated screens it
5416               	; 			' came out 46, 46, 70, 70, 70 px. Screens 2 and 4 flank the
5417               	; 			' elevator, so the store had a visibly tighter pair on one
5418               	; 			' side of it than the other, which is exactly how it was
5419               	; 			' reported from play.
5420               	; 			'
5421               	; 			' Both numbers are too small, which is the real fault. Kelly
5422               	; 			' closes on an oncoming hazard at WALKSP + the hazard's own
5423               	; 			' speed = 6 px a pass, and a jump lasts about 30 passes, so
5424               	; 			' a jump eats ~120 px of closing distance. At 46 or 70 there
5425               	; 			' is NO screen where he can land between the two: he clears
5426               	; 			' the first and comes down on the second.
5427               	; 			'
5428               	; 			' HAZGAP is that distance with margin. The first hazard
5429               	; 			' still lands where the table puts it, so the placement
5430               	; 			' variety is unchanged; only the pairing is now a fact about
5431               	; 			' the jump rather than an accident of two bytes.
5432               	; 			' assets/checkspace.py measures it and fails the build.
5433               	; 			' THE SECOND SLOT IS MEASURED FROM THE FIRST, not from its
5434               	; 			' own table byte. Adding HAZGAP to its own `lx AND 63` would
5435               	; 			' still leave the GAP varying by up to 63 px between screens
5436               	; 			' -- the same fault in a politer form, and the reason the two
5437               	; 			' sides of the elevator differed in the first place. Holding
5438               	; 			' slot 0's stagger and adding to that makes the gap exactly
5439               	; 			' HAZGAP on every screen, while slot 0 still lands wherever
5440               	; 			' the table puts it, so the placement variety is untouched.
5441               	; 			stag = lx AND 63

5521 BE9A D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5522               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
5523               	; 			'
5524               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
5525               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
5526               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
5527               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
5528               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
5529               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
5530               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
5531               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
5532               	; 			'
5533               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
5534               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
5535               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
5536               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
5537               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
5538               	; 			'
5539               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
5540               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
5541               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
5542               	; 			'
5543               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
5544               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
5545               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
5546               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
5547               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
5548               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
5549               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31
5550 BE9C D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0

6418               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
6419               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
6420               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
6421               	; 		'
6422               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
6423               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
6424               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
6425               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
6426               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
6427               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
6428               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
6429               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
6430               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
6431               	; 		'
6432               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
6433               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
6434               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
6435               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
6436               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
6437               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1
6438 C5AC D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESY,r0

6745               	;
6746               	; 	' --- running
6747               	; 	' THE END WALLS ARE MOVEMENT LIMITS, NOT A CLAMP APPLIED AFTERWARDS.
6748               	; 	'
6749               	; 	' They used to be the latter, and that is the whole bug: on screen 7 Kelly
6750               	; 	' walked on to x = 254, the crossing routine then noticed there was no
6751               	; 	' screen 8 and set him to 232 -- a 22 px snap BACKWARDS. He reached the
6752               	; 	' wall and was thrown off it. Testing the limit BEFORE the step means he
6753               	; 	' arrives at the wall and stays there, which is what a wall does.
6754               	; 	' IN THE AIR HE IS BALLISTIC. The horizontal direction is fixed at
6755               	; 	' take-off and the stick is ignored until he lands: steering in mid-air
6756               	; 	' is not a jump, it is flight, and it also let him flip which way he was
6757               	; 	' facing halfway through an arc. kjdx is 0 for a standing jump, 1 for a
6758               	; 	' running one to the right, 2 to the left.

6967               cv365
6968               	; 		' THE FIRST STEP OFF THE MARK IS ALWAYS HEARD. sfw is a distance --
6969               	; 		' a step every fifteen pixels -- so a short tap of the stick moved
6970               	; 		' him a few pixels and made NO sound at all. That does not read as
6971               	; 		' a short step, it reads as the controls being ignored, and it is
6972               	; 		' worst exactly where a player taps rather than holds: lining up a
6973               	; 		' jump, or edging around a hazard.
6974               	; 		'
6975               	; 		' Priming the accumulator to the threshold makes the very next pass
6976               	; 		' fire. It does not cheat the RATE: the trigger subtracts fifteen
6977               	; 		' rather than zeroing, so the pixels actually travelled still carry
6978               	; 		' into the following step and a held run comes out at 6.9 a second
6979               	; 		' either way.
6980               	; 		IF kmvl = 0 THEN sfw = 15
6981 C9C2 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KMVL,r0
     C9C4 2205

     C9CA C9D4

     C9CE 0F00

     C9D2 2208     
6987               cv366
6988               	; 		' AN ODOMETER, NOT A CLOCK -- pixels travelled, exactly like
6989               	; 		' Harry's hanim. It counted PASSES once, which made his legs cycle
6990               	; 		' slower on the escalator screens while he still ran at the same
6991               	; 		' speed; that was fixed by counting the frame DELTA instead, which
6992               	; 		' traded one mismatch for another -- his legs then kept wall-clock
6993               	; 		' time while his body still moved once per pass, so his stride
6994               	; 		' slipped against the ground whenever the loop was busy.
6995               	; 		'
6996               	; 		' Distance settles it. A pose per 8 px at any frame rate, and a
6997               	; 		' footstep is a stride rather than a tick.
6998               	; 		kanim = kanim + kspd

7113               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
7114               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
7115               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
7116               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
7117               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
7118               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
7119               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
7120               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
7121               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
7122               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
7123               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
7124               	; 	'
7125               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
7126               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
7127               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
7128               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
7129               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.

7765               	;
7766               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
7767               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is
7768               	; 	' drawn -- and at one 16x16 sprite it could only ever be one colour and
7769               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
7770               	; 	'
7771               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
7772               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
7773               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off
7774               	; 	' the screens that carry hazards).
7775               	; 	'
7776               	; 	' rdall = 1 paints all four cells, 0 repaints only the top-right one --
7777               	; 	' the pulse. Same address arithmetic either way, so the two cannot drift.
7778               	; 	' A BEAM TOP HANGS OUT OF THE FLOOR ABOVE, and the beam it belongs to has
7779               	; 	' just been taken away. `beam_tops` stamps SLABP -- the floor bar with the
7780               	; 	' support carried up through its lower three pixel rows -- into the slab
7781               	; 	' row above every pillar, and it runs BEFORE the radios and prizes are
7782               	; 	' placed. Clearing a pillar's own cells therefore left three grey pixels
7783               	; 	' dangling from the ceiling with nothing under them.
7784               	; 	'
7785               	; 	' TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: only SLABP is put back to SLAB. The row above the
7786               	; 	' top shopping floor is the ROOF DECK, a different character altogether,
7787               	; 	' and writing a floor bar over it would punch a hole in the roof.
7788               	; 	' A SHELF RUN IS FIVE CELLS WIDE and a radio covers two, so one standing
7789               	; 	' on a counter punched a hole through its middle and left the ends either
7790               	; 	' side -- the same incoherence the dangling pillar had, and worse, because
7791               	; 	' a counter reads as one object. The whole unit goes: walk outward along
7792               	; 	' the band's row 2 while the cell is still a shelf top, clearing it and
7793               	; 	' its bottom half together.
7794               	; 	'
7795               	; 	' Bounded at eight steps. The runs are five, so eight is slack rather
7796               	; 	' than a guess -- and an unbounded walk would run off the end of the row
7797               	; 	' if the name table ever held something unexpected.
7798               	; wipe_shelf:

8435               	; 	' ======================================================================
8436               	; 	' OBSTACLES

8438               	; upd_obst:
8439               cvb_UPD_OBST
8440               	; 	' ONE ACCUMULATOR PER KIND, NOT PER SLOT. Every hazard of a kind moves at
8441               	; 	' that kind's speed, so three calls a pass serve all eight slots.
8442               	; 	'
8443               	; 	' Per-slot would have meant up to eight calls of O(fdv) work every pass --
8444               	; 	' the positive feedback loop CLAUDE.md 3A warns about, where a slow pass is
8445               	; 	' made slower by the very code meant to take the loop rate out of the
8446               	; 	' game's behaviour. It also saves the eight bytes an obacc() would cost,
8447               	; 	' and ColecoVision RAM is the tighter budget.
8448               	; 	pacc = oacb
8449 D44C D820  54 	movb @cvb_OACB,@cvb_PACC
     D44E 20C7     
     D450 211E     
8450               	; 	psp64 = obsp

     D454 213B

     D49C 211E     
     D49E 20C9     
8479               	; 	ospp = pspd
8480 D4A0 D820  54 	movb @cvb_PSPD,@cvb_OSPP
     D4A2 2142     
     D4A4 20F3     
8481               	; 	' THE BOUNCE PHASE, ON THE SAME CLOCK AS THE JUMP AND AT ITS OLD PERIOD.
8482               	; 	'
8483               	; 	' The jump has always advanced by the frame delta (`kjf = kjf + fdv`) while
8484               	; 	' the bounce advanced once per PASS -- so whether a jump cleared a ball
8485               	; 	' depended on how busy the screen was, and the frame-by-frame property
8486               	; 	' checkball.py proves was exact only at fdv = 1.
8487               	; 	'
8488               	; 	' Stepping the phase by fdv directly would fix the clock and wreck the
8489               	; 	' game: a 32-phase arc would drop from about 1.35 s to 0.55 s, balls
8490               	; 	' bouncing 2.4x faster everywhere. So it gets a fraction of its own --
8491               	; 	' 25/64 of a phase per frame is 23.4 phases a second, against the 23.8 it
8492               	; 	' runs at today. Same bounce, now the same clock as the thing that has to
8493               	; 	' clear it.
8494               	; 	pacc = obac
8495 D4A6 D820  54 	movb @cvb_OBAC,@cvb_PACC
     D4A8 20E6     
     D4AA 211E

     D4AE 1900

8559               	; 				' THEY WRAP. They do not turn round at the wall.

8712               	; 	' ONE STEP OF A FRACTIONAL SPEED, FOR WHOEVER IS MOVING.
8713               	; 	'
8714               	; 	' `pacc` in and out, `psp64` in (sixty-fourths of a pixel per FRAME),
8715               	; 	' `pspd` out (whole pixels this pass). CVBasic has no locals, so the caller
8716               	; 	' stages its own accumulator in and takes it back out; that costs four
8717               	; 	' statements against the fourteen this used to be, written out per actor.
8718               	; 	'
8719               	; 	' Harry had the only copy. Kelly, the hazards and the bounce phase all need
8720               	; 	' the same thing -- everything the round clock judges has to advance by
8721               	; 	' ELAPSED FRAMES, or the loop rate becomes a difficulty dial and where the
8722               	; 	' player stands decides the outcome (DESIGN.md 0f-ter).
8723               	; 	'
8724               	; 	' TWO DRAINS CAP THE STEP AT 2 PER FRAME whatever psp64 says (CLAUDE.md
8725               	; 	' 3A: N drains cap the speed at N, and the surplus leaks into a byte that
8726               	; 	' wraps). Every caller is under that: Kelly 102/64 = 1.6, the fastest
8727               	; 	' hazard 76/64 = 1.2, Harry 57/64 = 0.9, the bounce phase 25/64 = 0.4.
8728               	; 	'
8729               	; 	' FIVE FRAMES, AND THREE WAS TOO FEW. At 2 px a frame five frames is 10 px,
8730               	; 	' and to skip a position window an actor must start outside it on one side
8731               	; 	' and land outside on the other, which needs 12. Three was the original bug
8732               	; 	' in miniature: the lift screen runs at almost exactly 20 passes a second,
8733               	; 	' which IS fdv = 3, so every hitch there lost a frame. The clamp exists for
8734               	; 	' a genuine stall and at five it only fires for one.
8735               	; pace_step:
8736               cvb_PACE_STEP
8737               	; 	pfd = fdv

9221               	;
9222               	; 	' THE SIXTEENTH-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen
9223               	; 	' (2), so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here
9224               	; 	' and is spent as whole pixels. THREE IFs, not a loop and not a divide:
9225               	; 	' `%` and `/` both compile to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this
9226               	; 	' runs every pass.
9227               	; 	'
9228               	; 	' THE NUMBER OF DRAINS IS A CEILING ON THE SPEED, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE
9229               	; 	' POINT OF THIS COMMENT. Each IF can spend one whole pixel, so N drains
9230               	; 	' cap hspd at N px per pass NO MATTER WHAT hsp64 SAYS. The invariant is:
9231               	; 	'
9232               	; 	'     hacc is under 16 on entry, and hsp64 is at most 48,
9233               	; 	'     so hacc is under 64 here and three drains always suffice.
9234               	; 	'
9235               	; 	' KEEP hsp64 <= 48. This was quarter-pixels with TWO drains and the same
9236               	; 	' invariant written the same way -- "hsp4 is at most 8, so it can never
9237               	; 	' need a third" -- and it was correct until somebody set hsp4 = 9 without
9238               	; 	' re-reading it. The crook then ran at a flat 2.0 px/pass while every
9239               	; 	' comment, the design table and assets/checkchase.py all said 2.25, and
9240               	; 	' the surplus quarter leaked into hacc and wrapped the byte every 256
9241               	; 	' passes. Nothing failed; he simply arrived two thirds of a screen short
9242               	; 	' of his own escape. checkchase.py now counts these IFs and refuses a
9243               	; 	' speed the drains cannot deliver.
9244               	; 	'
9245               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
9246               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
9247               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
9248               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
9249               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
9250               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
9251               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
9252               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
9253               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
9254               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
9255               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
9256               	; 	' flight looking busy.
9257               	; 	'
9258               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
9259               	; 	'
9260               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the
9261               	; 	' flee as well as the climb, so for the whole of the run he held one
9262               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
9263               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
9264               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
9265               	; 	'
9266               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,
9267               	; 	' which expired somewhere in the middle of a floor and turned him round
9268               	; 	' for no reason the player could see. Running until the wall means every
9269               	; 	' change of direction has something on screen behind it.
9270               	; 	IF hrun > 0 THEN

9351               cv589
9352               	;
9353               	; 	' HE WALKS BY REAL TIME, NOT BY LOOP PASS -- one accumulate-and-drain per
9354               	; 	' ELAPSED FRAME rather than per pass. Everything that moves here used to be
9355               	; 	' per pass while the CLOCK is paced by the frame delta, so a busy screen
9356               	; 	' slowed Harry in real seconds and the clock did not slow with him: the
9357               	; 	' same uninterrupted escape finished with nine timer units in hand from a
9358               	; 	' light screen and none at all from the lift screen, decided by where the
9359               	; 	' player happened to be standing. Now he covers the same ground per second
9360               	; 	' wherever anybody is (DESIGN.md 0f-ter).
9361               	; 	'
9362               	; 	' A LOOP, NOT A MULTIPLY. `*` compiles to a real TMS9900 MPY, and reading a
9363               	; 	' 16-bit variable straight after one returns the product's HIGH word
9364               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A). fdv is 1..3 here, so this is at most three adds.
9365               	; 	'
9366               	; 	' SIXTY-FOURTHS, AND ONLY TWO DRAINS -- both fall out of accumulating per
9367               	; 	' FRAME instead of per pass. He covers under a pixel a frame, so two drains
9368               	; 	' are ample where the per-pass version needed three; and the finer unit is
9369               	; 	' what makes the speed tunable at all. In sixteenths one step was about six
9370               	; 	' SECONDS of his route, so nothing landed near the buzzer; in sixty-fourths
9371               	; 	' it is about one and a half. Keep hsp64 <= 128: hacc is under 64 on entry,
9372               	; 	' so 64 + 128 stays inside a byte and two drains always suffice.
9373               	; 	'
9374               	; 	' AND THE STEP IS CLAMPED TO THREE FRAMES' WORTH, WHICH IS NOT A DETAIL.
9375               	; 	' Two tests sample his position once a pass and neither interpolates:
9376               	; 	'
9377               	; 	'   ARRIVAL   `hdx < 6` around the escalator or the roof door -- an ELEVEN
9378               	; 	'             pixel window. A step over 10 px can jump clean across it and
9379               	; 	'             he never arrives at all.
9380               	; 	'   THE CATCH `hdd < CATCHR` is 12, a 23-wide band, and Kelly closes 4 of
9381               	; 	'             it himself -- so Harry over ~18 px could pass through the Kop
9382               	; 	'             between two passes without either sample being inside it.
9383               	; 	'
9384               	; 	' The clamp and the drains live in pace_step now, with the reasoning; the
9385               	; 	' numbers above are what sized them. Measured: TIME 02 on the lift screen
9386               	; 	' against TIME 04 on a light one when the clamp was 3, and 00 against 09
9387               	; 	' before any of this.
9388               	; 	'
9389               	; 	' The ESCALATOR RIDE is deliberately untouched: it returns above this,
9390               	; 	' clocked one step per pass to stay locked to the moving staircase. A rider
9391               	; 	' advanced by the frame delta either drifts off the treads or makes the
9392               	; 	' steps run backwards (CLAUDE.md 3A, DESIGN.md 0f). It is about 3% of his
9393               	; 	' journey.
9394               	; 	'
9395               	; 	' `hanim` still advances by hspd and is still an ODOMETER -- pixels
9396               	; 	' travelled, not time -- so the run cycle stays locked to the ground at any
9397               	; 	' rate. A 9 px step moves it exactly one pose of its 8 px beat.
9398               	; 	pacc = hacc
9399 DAEA D820  54 	movb @cvb_HACC,@cvb_PACC
     DAEC 21A9     
     DAEE 211E     
9400               	; 	psp64 = hsp64

     DAF2 20D3     
     DAF4 2126

     DAF8 A980     
9404 DAFA D686     	data cvb_PACE_STEP

     DAFE 211E

9561               	; 	' THE CYCLE ADVANCES BY PIXELS TRAVELLED, NOT BY TIME. This was `+ fdv`,
9562               	; 	' which is a clock -- and Harry's speed is a QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR, so
9563               	; 	' hspd is 0 on a third of his frames. On every one of those the legs took
9564               	; 	' another step while the ground did not move, which is precisely what
9565               	; 	' scrubbing your feet looks like. It is the classic foot-slip: the legs
9566               	; 	' and the world running off two different clocks.
9567               	; 	'
9568               	; 	' Advancing by hspd locks the stride to the floor -- he cannot take a step
9569               	; 	' without covering ground, or cover ground without taking one, at any
9570               	; 	' speed, including the frames where the accumulator gives him nothing.

9827 DE4A C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
9828 DE4C 0450  20 	b *r0
9829               	;
9830               	; 	' ======================================================================
9831               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
9832               	; 	' ======================================================================
9833               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
9834               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
9835               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
9836               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
9837               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
9838               	; 	'
9839               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
9840               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
9841               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
9842               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
9843               	; 	' broken.
9844               	; 	'
9845               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
9846               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
9847               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
9848               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
9849               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
9850               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
9851               	; 	'
9852               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
9853               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
9854               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
9855               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
9856               	; coll_obst:
9857               cvb_COLL_OBST
9858               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
9859 DE4E D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0

     E0CC 2098     
10155               	; 	' ONE PASS LONGER THAN THE SOUND, because the sound does not start here.
10156               	; 	' sfh is a latch that sfx_tick consumes on the NEXT pass, so the note
10157               	; 	' runs passes 2..HITSND+1 of the freeze and this ends with it.
10158               	; 	hfz = HITSND + 1
10159 E0CE 0200  20 	li r0,5376   ; 21*256
     E0D0 1500     
10160 E0D2 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_HFZ
     E0D4 218A     
10161               	; 	' THE FLOOR CLEARS WHEN YOU ARE HIT, and stays clear until you re-enter
10162               	; 	' the screen. Nine seconds is a heavy penalty on a fifty-unit clock, and
10163               	; 	' taking it while still standing among the things that charged it -- with
10164               	; 	' a second hazard 48 px behind the first -- is how one mistake becomes
10165               	; 	' three. Clearing the row makes the penalty a single event you can walk
10166               	; 	' away from.
10167               	; 	'
10168               	; 	' BOTH SLOTS, WHATEVER IS IN THEM. Radios used to be exempt: they are
10169               	; 	' CHARACTERS stamped into the name table rather than sprites, so zeroing
10170               	; 	' the kind would have stopped them colliding while leaving them plainly
10171               	; 	' visible on the shelf, and a fixture that is still there has to still be
10172               	; 	' there. That was a reason to UNDRAW them, not a reason to keep them --
10173               	; 	' and it left the one hazard that cannot leave on its own as the only one
10174               	; 	' the mercy did not cover. haz_off now takes the characters with it.
10175               	; 	'
10176               	; 	' NOTHING RESTORES IT HERE. load_band repopulates the band from the
10177               	; 	' template, and it runs only on a seam crossing or a round start, so
10178               	; 	' "until the screen is re-entered" costs no state and no timer: leave and
10179               	; 	' come back and the hazards are simply placed again.
10180               	; 	'
10181               	; 	' THE CALL ITSELF IS IN THE MAIN LOOP, at the end of the freeze -- see
10182               	; 	' the hfz block there for why.

10393               	; draw_actors:

10395               	; 	' RIDING WITH THE DOORS SHUT: HIDE HIM AND DO NOT DRAW HIM AT ALL.
10396               	; 	'
10397               	; 	' This test used to sit AFTER his sprites had been written, so every pass
10398               	; 	' placed him at his old floor and then hid him again. That is not free.
10399               	; 	' CVBasic's SPRITE writes a RAM mirror which the vblank ISR copies to
10400               	; 	' VRAM, so a vblank landing BETWEEN the draw and the hide latches the
10401               	; 	' visible state for a frame -- and draw_actors is long (Kelly, Harry,
10402               	; 	' eight obstacles) while a pass on the lift screen spans two or three
10403               	; 	' frames, so it happened constantly. He flickered at the floor he had
10404               	; 	' just left, which reads as a drawing bug rather than as a journey.
10405               	; 	'
10406               	; 	' Deciding BEFORE drawing costs one test and cannot race the ISR at all:
10407               	; 	' the mirror never holds a position to be caught with.
10408               	; 	IF klst = ST_ELEV THEN

10486               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
10487               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
10488               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
10489               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
10490               	; 	'
10491               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
10492               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
10493               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
10494               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
10495               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
10496               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
10497               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.
10498               	; 	ky = flry(klv)

10534               	;
10535               	; 	' RIDING, HE STANDS ON THE STEP. The lip is two pixels proud of the shop
10536               	; 	' floor, so a rider whose feet stayed at floor level would be standing

10642               	; 		' FOUR RUN FRAMES FROM TWO BITS of the animation counter, and no
10643               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
10644               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.
10645               	; 		'
10646               	; 		' KELLY USES BITS 2 AND 3 AND HARRY USES 3 AND 4, because a stride
10647               	; 		' has to cover about a stride's worth of ground or the figure
10648               	; 		' skates. Kelly runs 4 px a frame, so four frames a pose is 16 px;
10649               	; 		' Harry runs 1.75, so eight frames a pose is 14. Matching the two
10650               	; 		' rates to the two speeds is what keeps both looking like running.
10651               	; 		kq = P_KLEG1
10652 E452 0200  20 	li r0,14336   ; 56*256
     E454 3800     
10653 E456 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_KQ
     E458 21D8     
10654               	; 		' BITS 3 AND 4, NOT 2 AND 3 -- A POSE EVERY 8 PIXELS, which is
10655               	; 		' Harry's beat (hanim AND 8 / AND 16) and now means the same thing,
10656               	; 		' because both counters hold pixels travelled. On the old 4-count
10657               	; 		' beat an odometer would have flipped his legs every single pass at
10658               	; 		' 4 px a pass, which is a blur rather than a run. checkanim reads
10659               	; 		' these bits out of the source, and fails if two bands of one figure
10660               	; 		' run on different ones -- so the body below moves with them.
10661               	; 		IF kanim AND 8 THEN kq = kq + 4
10662 E45A D020  34 	movb @cvb_KANIM,r0
     E45C 20FD

10845               	; 	' BITS 3 AND 4: one pose per 8 px of travel, a 32 px four-beat cycle.
10846               	; 	' `hanim` advances by `hspd`, which is PIXELS MOVED THIS PASS, so a pose
10847               	; 	' is a DISTANCE and not a duration -- it cannot drift with the frame rate
10848               	; 	' or scrub his feet at any speed.
10849               	; 	'
10850               	; 	' HALVING THIS WAS TRIED AND IS WORSE. Bits 4 and 5 put a pose on 16 px
10851               	; 	' and the cycle on 64, which is closer to a real stride for a figure 24 px
10852               	; 	' tall and was rejected on sight. Do not re-derive it from his height; the
10853               	; 	' faster cadence is what the reference reads like at this size.
10854               	; 	IF hanim AND 8 THEN hq = hq + 4

10889               	; 	' HIS ARMS SWING, on the same bit of the animation counter that picks the
10890               	; 	' legs -- so the arm that is forward is the one opposite the leading leg,
10891               	; 	' which is what running looks like.
10892               	; 	' THE ARMS SWING ONCE PER FULL LEG CYCLE, ON BIT 16 -- not on bit 8.
10893               	; 	'
10894               	; 	' Bit 8 is one BEAT, and the legs take four of them (bits 8 and 16). So an
10895               	; 	' arm swing keyed to bit 8 completed twice for every one pass of the legs:
10896               	; 	' the top half of him flapping at double the speed of the bottom half.
10897               	; 	' That is the jig. It is not a drawing problem and no amount of redrawing
10898               	; 	' the legs would have touched it.
10899               	; 	'
10900               	; 	' On bit 16 the arms hold through beats 1-2 and swap for 3-4, which is
10901               	; 	' also the right PHASE: beat 1 leads with one leg and beat 3 with the
10902               	; 	' other, so the arm opposite the leading leg is forward in each -- which
10903               	; 	' is what running looks like.
10904               	; 	' THE TORSO RUNS ON THE SAME TWO BITS AS THE LEGS. With four drawn poses
10905               	; 	' the shoulders and arms swing WITH the stride; the old two-frame body had
10906               	; 	' a clock of its own, which is what read as a flap rather than a run.
10907               	; 	hp = P_HBODY

10983               	; 	' HE IS STRIPED FROM CAP TO HEM. Both stripe colours run the whole upper
10984               	; 	' half, so both boxes span rows 0-15 and neither can be tucked away --
10985               	; 	' affordable only because Kelly's hat moved into a box of its own. His
10986               	; 	' FACE is pushed DOWN instead (hy+3, box 3..18) so it clears the cap
10987               	; 	' rows, where his two stripe boxes plus Kelly's hat and face already
10988               	; 	' make four. Worst line of a meeting: exactly four, nothing dropped.
10989               	; 	' HE RISES A PIXEL ON THE RECOVERY BEATS. A runner is highest at mid-flight
10990               	; 	' and lowest at contact; with the body pinned at a constant y, alternating
10991               	; 	' legs under a motionless torso reads as a dance rather than as travel.
10992               	; 	'
10993               	; 	' One pixel is enough at this size, and it lifts the BODY ONLY -- the legs
10994               	; 	' keep their y, so the planted foot stays on the floor and the figure
10995               	; 	' stretches at the waist instead of hopping. Lifting the legs too would be
10996               	; 	' a jump.
10997               	; 	'
10998               	; 	' Beats 2 and 4 are the recoveries, which is exactly `hanim AND 8` -- the
10999               	; 	' cycle's LOW beat bit. IT MUST MOVE WITH THE BEAT BITS ABOVE IT: on the
11000               	; 	' wrong bit he rises and falls twice per stride, a second clock inside one
11001               	; 	' figure, which is the flapping of DESIGN.md 0j2 in the vertical.
11002               	; 	'
11003               	; 	' Safe because HARRY'S COLLISION HAS NO VERTICAL COMPONENT: he is caught on
11004               	; 	' `same level AND |dx| < CATCHR`. Doing this to Kelly would desynchronise
11005               	; 	' his sprite from the ball and biplane windows in DESIGN.md 5a, which is
11006               	; 	' why it is not done to him.
11007               	; 	' THE WHOLE FIGURE RISES, legs included, rather than the body stretching
11008               	; 	' away from the feet. Two reasons, and the second was not the first plan:
11009               	; 	'
11010               	; 	'   * It is what a run does. Mid-flight BOTH feet are off the ground, so
11011               	; 	'     lifting the legs with the body is the correct pose, not a compromise.
11012               	; 	'   * Lifting the body alone opens a one-pixel gap at the waist, and the
11013               	; 	'     torso's bottom row and the legs' top row do not align across it --
11014               	; 	'     green shows through and he reads as coming apart.
11015               	; 	'
11016               	; 	' It also keeps `hfy = hy - 7` and `hy2 = hy + 16` LITERAL, which is not a
11017               	; 	' cosmetic concern: assets/checkbands.py reads each band's draw offset by
11018               	; 	' parsing those exact expressions. Bobbing a differently-named variable
11019               	; 	' made them unreadable and the gate failed the build -- correctly. A
11020               	; 	' checker that can no longer see what it checks has to fail, not shrug.

11488 EAD4 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11489 EAD6 0450  20 	b *r0
11490               	;
11491               	; 	' ======================================================================
11492               	; 	' THE SCANNER
11493               	; 	' ======================================================================
11494               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
11495               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
11496               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
11497               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
11498               	; 	'
11499               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
11500               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
11501               	; 	'
11502               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
11503               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
11504               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
11505               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
11506               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
11507               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
11508               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
11509               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
11510               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
11511               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
11512               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
11513               	; esc_tick:
11514               cvb_ESC_TICK
11515               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
11516               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
11517               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
11518               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11519               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
11520 EAD8 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EADA 219A     
11521 EADC 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     EADE 0100     
11522 EAE0 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11523 EAE2 1402  14 	jhe cv804
11524 EAE4 0460  28 	b @cv803
     EAE6 EAFC     
11525               cv804
11526               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
11527 EAE8 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EAEA 219A     
11528 EAEC 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     EAEE 0700     
11529 EAF0 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11530 EAF2 1A02  14 	jl cv806
11531 EAF4 0460  28 	b @cv805
     EAF6 EAFC     
11532               cv806
11533 EAF8 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11534 EAFA 0450  20 	b *r0
11535               cv805
11536               	; 	END IF
11537               cv803
11538               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
11539               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
11540               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
11541               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
11542               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
11543               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
11544               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
11545               	; 	'
11546               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
11547               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
11548               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
11549               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
11550               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
11551               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
11552               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
11553               	; 	escp = escp + 1
11554 EAFC D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     EAFE 20B1     
11555 EB00 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     EB02 0100

11565               cv807
11566               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
11567               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
11568               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
11569               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
11570               	; 	' 7 an east one.
11571               	; 	'
11572               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
11573               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
11574               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
11575               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
11576               	; 	'
11577               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
11578               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
11579               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
11580               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
11581               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
11582               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
11583               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
11584               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.
11585               	; 	'
11586               	; 	' The handrail and the frame sit past the end of both ranges and never
11587               	; 	' move at all.

11847 ED56 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11848 ED58 0450  20 	b *r0
11849               	;
11850               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
11851               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
11852               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
11853               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
11854               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
11855               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
11856               	; 	'
11857               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
11858               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
11859               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
11860               	; 	'
11861               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
11862               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
11863               	; 	'   +2  GREY
11864               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
11865               	; 	'
11866               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
11867               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
11868               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
11869               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
11870               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
11871               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
11872               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
11873               	; 	'
11874               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
11875               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
11876               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
11877               	; 	'
11878               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
11879               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
11880               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
11881               	; scan_furn:
11882               cvb_SCAN_FURN
11883               	; 	FOR fl = 0 TO 3
